tables/mart__alerts_per_job/mart__alerts_per_job.hql

Show its 37 lines
-- The Hive of mart.alerts_per_job, generated by export_hive() from the logic cell of
-- mart__alerts_per_job.ipynb, beside this file. Edit the logic there, not this file:
-- each export_hive() writes it again, from the notebook as last saved. It holds 2 steps,
-- in the order they are sent: create() and write().

-- 1 of 2: create()
CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mart.alerts_per_job (
  job_id BIGINT,
  alerts BIGINT,
  high_alerts BIGINT,
  PRIMARY KEY (job_id, dt) DISABLE NOVALIDATE
)
PARTITIONED BY (
  dt STRING
)
STORED AS ORC
LOCATION '/data/your_team/mart.db/alerts_per_job';

-- 2 of 2: write()
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.alerts_per_job PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  job_runs.job_id,
  COUNT(*) AS alerts,
  COUNT(CASE WHEN run_alerts.severity = 'high' THEN 1 END) AS high_alerts,
  run_alerts.dt
FROM ops.run_alerts AS run_alerts
JOIN ops.job_runs AS job_runs
  ON job_runs.run_id = run_alerts.run_id
WHERE
  run_alerts.dt = '2026-09-24'
  AND job_runs.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-23' AND '2026-09-24'
GROUP BY
  job_runs.job_id,
  run_alerts.dt;

tables/mart__alerts_per_job/mart__alerts_per_job.ipynb

mart.alerts_per_job

Each job's alerts and high alerts per day, saved every day.

Why: an alert names only its run, so finding its job means joining every alert to ops.job_runs, which is worth doing once a day and saving, rather than in every Statement that counts alerts.

Made by project.new_table("mart.alerts_per_job") from admin/templates/saved_table.py, then filled in. Its rows join each alert to its run, for the run's job, so READS filters both tables. A run can raise an alert after midnight, so ops.job_runs is read from the day before as well: an alert counts on the day it was raised, whichever of the two days its run is in. Each alert joins its run on run_id, ops.job_runs' Key, so it is counted once.

The code cells: 1 the Project and your query, connected by connect(...); 2 the logic, which starts # logic; 3 try it; 4 its lineage; 5 its Hive, in mart__alerts_per_job.hql; 6 its rows in the Reference sheet.

Code cell 2

# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the rows it reads, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, INSERT_OVERWRITE, JOIN, SELECT, WHERE, Clause, Statement, all_of,
    between, count_rows, create_table, equals, statement,
)
from references.mart__alerts_per_job import alerts_per_job
from references.ops__job_runs import job_runs
from references.ops__run_alerts import high, run_alerts  # high: an Alias, severity is "high"

# The rows write() reads: change it to write others. It reads two tables, so it filters
# both: the alerts raised on a day, and the runs of that day and the day before, since a run
# can raise an alert after midnight.
READS = all_of(equals(run_alerts.dt, "2026-09-24"),
               between(job_runs.dt, "2026-09-23", "2026-09-24"))

# This table's partition columns, in the order its SELECT gives them, last: [] for none.
PARTITIONED_BY = ["dt"]


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.alerts_per_job from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(alerts_per_job, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses preview and write share: each job's alerts for each dt of the alerts READS
    picks. dt comes last in SELECT, as run_alerts.dt, the day each alert was raised:
    INSERT_OVERWRITE writes the rows into the Partitions their dt names."""
    return [
        SELECT(job_runs.job_id, AS(count_rows(), "alerts"),
               AS(count_rows(where=high), "high_alerts"), run_alerts.dt),
        FROM(run_alerts),
        JOIN(job_runs, ON=equals(job_runs.run_id, run_alerts.run_id)),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(job_runs.job_id, run_alerts.dt),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the rows, replacing whatever the Partitions they fill held."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(alerts_per_job), *rows())

tables/mart__alerts_per_severity/mart__alerts_per_severity.ipynb

mart.alerts_per_severity

One row per severity and day: how many alerts of that severity the runs raised that day.

A Saved table worked out from a table it reads: a count per group of the rows READS picks. It is partitioned as PARTITIONED_BY says, or not at all.

Made by project.new_table("mart.alerts_per_severity") from admin/templates/saved_table.py, which filled in the database, the table and its file's name, mart__alerts_per_severity. Run the cells top to bottom once both are filled in.

The code cells: 1 the Project and your query, connected by connect(...); 2 the logic, which starts # logic; 3 try it; 4 its lineage; 5 its Hive, in mart__alerts_per_severity.hql; 6 its rows in the Reference sheet. The logic only imports and defines, so project.load("mart.alerts_per_severity") can run it from anywhere else, for lineage across your tables and for admin/run_tables.ipynb, which sends its write().

Code cell 1

Code cell 2

# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the rows it reads, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, INSERT_OVERWRITE, SELECT, WHERE, Clause, Statement, count_rows,
    create_table, equals, statement,
)
from references.mart__alerts_per_severity import alerts_per_severity  # this table's own Table reference
# each table it reads, such as: from references.ops__job_runs import job_runs
from references.ops__run_alerts import run_alerts

# The rows write() reads: change it to write others. A condition that calls something other
# than equals, such as between or is_in, needs it added to the import above.
READS = equals(run_alerts.dt, "2026-09-24")

# This table's partition columns, in the order its SELECT gives them, last: [] for none.
PARTITIONED_BY = ["dt"]


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.alerts_per_severity from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(alerts_per_severity, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses preview and write share, so the two can never disagree: a count per group
    of the rows READS picks.

    A partitioned table's partition columns come last in SELECT, in its Table reference's
    order, named as it names them: INSERT_OVERWRITE fills each Partition from the rows' own
    values, and replaces only the Partitions they fill. They stay in GROUP_BY too, so each
    Partition's rows stay apart.
    """
    return [
        SELECT(
            run_alerts.severity,  # what each row is for
            AS(count_rows(), "alerts"),
            run_alerts.dt,  # this table's partition column, last
        ),
        FROM(run_alerts),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(
            run_alerts.severity,
            run_alerts.dt,  # kept, so each Partition's rows stay apart
        ),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the rows, replacing the Partitions they fill, or the whole table if it
    isn't partitioned: sent twice, it leaves the same rows, not twice as many."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(alerts_per_severity), *rows())

tables/mart__daily_job_runs/lineage/20260925-060000-mart__daily_job_runs.html

A Lineage page: open it, from the download's docs folder. Its Markdown twin, the .md file of the same name, holds the same text.

tables/mart__daily_job_runs/mart__daily_job_runs.hql

Show its 45 lines
-- The Hive of mart.daily_job_runs, generated by export_hive() from the logic cell of
-- mart__daily_job_runs.ipynb, beside this file. Edit the logic there, not this file:
-- each export_hive() writes it again, from the notebook as last saved. It holds 2 steps,
-- in the order they are sent: create() and write().

-- 1 of 2: create()
CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mart.daily_job_runs (
  job_id BIGINT,
  runs BIGINT,
  failed_runs BIGINT,
  minutes BIGINT,
  PRIMARY KEY (job_id, dt) DISABLE NOVALIDATE
)
PARTITIONED BY (
  dt STRING
)
STORED AS ORC
LOCATION '/data/your_team/mart.db/daily_job_runs';

-- 2 of 2: write()
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
WITH runs_per_job AS (
  SELECT
    job_runs.job_id,
    job_runs.dt,
    COUNT(*) AS runs,
    COUNT(CASE WHEN job_runs.status = 'FAILED' THEN 1 END) AS failed_runs,
    SUM(job_runs.duration_mins) AS minutes
  FROM ops.job_runs AS job_runs
  WHERE
    job_runs.dt = '2026-09-24'
  GROUP BY
    job_runs.job_id,
    job_runs.dt
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.daily_job_runs PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  runs_per_job.job_id,
  runs_per_job.runs,
  runs_per_job.failed_runs,
  runs_per_job.minutes,
  runs_per_job.dt
FROM runs_per_job;

tables/mart__daily_job_runs/mart__daily_job_runs.ipynb

mart.daily_job_runs

Each job's runs, failed runs and minutes per day, saved every day.

Why: counting every run again each time someone asks is slow, so the counts are worked out once a day and saved in a Saved table that anyone can read, mart.team_day among them.

Made by project.new_table("mart.daily_job_runs") from admin/templates/saved_table.py, then filled in. Where the Template counts the rows READS picks, its rows come from the Building block building_blocks/runs_per_job.py, given READS, which mart.team_day's notebook reads too, so the two can never count runs two ways. Every run counts, TEST runs among them. It is partitioned by dt, as ops.job_runs is.

The code cells: 1 the Project and your query, connected by connect(...); 2 the logic, which starts # logic; 3 try it; 4 its lineage; 5 its Hive, in mart__daily_job_runs.hql; 6 its rows in the Reference sheet. The Example database can be read but not written, so cell 3 prints each write's Hive and runs only the preview.

Code cell 2

# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the rows it reads, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    FROM, INSERT_OVERWRITE, SELECT, Clause, Statement, create_table, equals, statement,
)
from building_blocks.runs_per_job import runs_per_job
from references.mart__daily_job_runs import daily_job_runs
from references.ops__job_runs import job_runs

# The rows write() reads: change it to write others.
READS = equals(job_runs.dt, "2026-09-24")

# This table's partition columns, in the order its SELECT gives them, last: [] for none.
PARTITIONED_BY = ["dt"]


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.daily_job_runs from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet.
    may_exist=True writes CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS, so it never changes a table
    that is there."""
    return create_table(daily_job_runs, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses preview and write share: each job's numbers for each dt of the rows READS
    picks, from the Building block. dt comes last in SELECT: INSERT_OVERWRITE(daily_job_runs)
    writes the rows into the Partitions their dt names, PARTITION(dt), and replaces only
    those. Cell 3 prints the Hive it makes."""
    per_job = runs_per_job(READS)
    return [
        SELECT(per_job.job_id, per_job.runs, per_job.failed_runs, per_job.minutes, per_job.dt),
        FROM(per_job),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the rows, replacing whatever the Partitions they fill held, so sending it
    twice leaves the same rows, not twice as many."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(daily_job_runs), *rows())

-- 1 of 2
CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mart.daily_job_runs (
  job_id BIGINT,
  runs BIGINT,
  failed_runs BIGINT,
  minutes BIGINT,
  PRIMARY KEY (job_id, dt) DISABLE NOVALIDATE
)
PARTITIONED BY (
  dt STRING
)
STORED AS ORC
LOCATION '/data/your_team/mart.db/daily_job_runs';

-- 2 of 2
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
WITH runs_per_job AS (
  SELECT
    job_runs.job_id,
    job_runs.dt,
    COUNT(*) AS runs,
    COUNT(CASE WHEN job_runs.status = 'FAILED' THEN 1 END) AS failed_runs,
    SUM(job_runs.duration_mins) AS minutes
  FROM ops.job_runs AS job_runs
  WHERE
    job_runs.dt = '2026-09-24'
  GROUP BY
    job_runs.job_id,
    job_runs.dt
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.daily_job_runs PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  runs_per_job.job_id,
  runs_per_job.runs,
  runs_per_job.failed_runs,
  runs_per_job.minutes,
  runs_per_job.dt
FROM runs_per_job;

-- The Hive of mart.team_day, generated by export_hive() from the logic cell of
-- mart__team_day.ipynb, beside this file. Edit the logic there, not this file: each

CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mart.team_day (
  team STRING,
  runs BIGINT,
  failed_runs BIGINT,
  minutes BIGINT,
  PRIMARY KEY (team, dt) DISABLE NOVALIDATE
)
PARTITIONED BY (
  dt STRING
)
STORED AS ORC
LOCATION '/data/your_team/mart.db/team_day';

-- 2 of 2: write()
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.team_day PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  jobs.team,
  SUM(daily_job_runs.runs) AS runs,
  SUM(daily_job_runs.failed_runs) AS failed_runs,
  SUM(daily_job_runs.minutes) AS minutes,
  daily_job_runs.dt
FROM mart.daily_job_runs AS daily_job_runs
JOIN ops.jobs AS jobs
  ON jobs.job_id = daily_job_runs.job_id
WHERE
  daily_job_runs.dt = '2026-09-24'
GROUP BY
  jobs.team,
  daily_job_runs.dt;

tables/mart__team_day/mart__team_day.ipynb

mart.team_day

Each team's runs, failed runs and minutes per day, saved every day.

Why: once mart.daily_job_runs has saved a day, each team's numbers come from a few saved rows per job, not from every run again.

Made by project.new_table("mart.team_day") from admin/templates/saved_table.py, then filled in. It reads the Saved table mart.daily_job_runs through its Table reference, like any other table, and ops.jobs for each job's team, so it is written after mart.daily_job_runs: run_tables sends it last. Adding up the jobs' counts per team is safe: a sum of counts is the count of them all.

The Saved table is in your warehouse, not in the Example database, so its preview can't run here. team_runs_from_job_runs(...) can: it groups the Building block's rows by team the same way, from ops.job_runs itself. Its lineage, cell 4, draws both tables, from ops.job_runs to here, through mart.daily_job_runs' logic.

The code cells: 1 the Project and your query, connected by connect(...); 2 the logic, which starts # logic; 3 try it; 4 its lineage; 5 its Hive, in mart__team_day.hql; 6 its rows in the Reference sheet.

Code cell 1

# 1. The Project and your query

# Your connection to the warehouse. Each notebook runs on its own, so each connects here, at
# its top, before it calls query or execute: write yours in the line below and delete its #,
# such as connect(your_library.connect(...)). A connection kept in a file outside the Project,
# such as team_connection.py, is imported by these lines, written in that line's place:
#     sys.path.append("C:/work/connections")  # the folder team_connection.py is in

Code cell 2

# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the rows it reads, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, INSERT_OVERWRITE, JOIN, SELECT, WHERE, Clause, Condition, Statement,
    create_table, equals, statement, sum_of,
)
from building_blocks.runs_per_job import runs_per_job
from references.mart__daily_job_runs import daily_job_runs
from references.mart__team_day import team_day
from references.ops__job_runs import job_runs
from references.ops__jobs import jobs

# The rows write() reads: change it to write others.
READS = equals(daily_job_runs.dt, "2026-09-24")

# This table's partition columns, in the order its SELECT gives them, last: [] for none.
PARTITIONED_BY = ["dt"]


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.team_day from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(team_day, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses preview and write share: each team's numbers for each dt of the rows READS
    picks, from daily_job_runs, the table in FROM, whose dt comes last in SELECT:
    INSERT_OVERWRITE writes the rows into the Partitions their dt names."""
    return [
        SELECT(
            jobs.team,
            AS(sum_of(daily_job_runs.runs), "runs"),
            AS(sum_of(daily_job_runs.failed_runs), "failed_runs"),
            AS(sum_of(daily_job_runs.minutes), "minutes"),
            daily_job_runs.dt,
        ),
        FROM(daily_job_runs),
        JOIN(jobs, ON=equals(jobs.job_id, daily_job_runs.job_id)),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(jobs.team, daily_job_runs.dt),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the rows, once mart.daily_job_runs has written them."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(team_day), *rows())


def team_runs_from_job_runs(reads: Condition) -> Statement:
    """Each team's runs, failed runs and minutes for the rows of ops.job_runs `reads` picks,
    such as equals(job_runs.dt, "2026-09-24"), through the Building block mart.daily_job_runs
    saves: so it runs on the Example database, and at work checks a day mart.daily_job_runs
    hasn't saved yet."""
    per_job = runs_per_job(reads)
    return statement(
        SELECT(
            jobs.team,
            AS(sum_of(per_job.runs), "runs"),
            AS(sum_of(per_job.failed_runs), "failed_runs"),
            AS(sum_of(per_job.minutes), "minutes"),
        ),
        FROM(per_job),
        JOIN(jobs, ON=equals(jobs.job_id, per_job.job_id)),
        GROUP_BY(jobs.team),
    )

It shows nothing.

run_query(team_runs_from_job_runs(equals(job_runs.dt, "2026-09-24")), query)

warehouse.py

Show its 86 lines
"""warehouse - connect, which takes your connection, and its two calls.

Why: every notebook in the Project reaches the warehouse through query and execute, so a change
to how you reach it is made here, once.

Each notebook runs on its own, so each gives its connection at its top, in its setup cell, with
connect(your_connection), before it calls query or execute; until it does, they refuse. Your
connection is an object whose query(sql) runs Hive that gives back rows and gives them back as
a pandas DataFrame, and whose execute(sql) runs Hive that changes something: whatever it gives
back, execute drops. You pick which of query and execute sends each Statement, by calling
run_query(s, query) for what gives back rows, as a SELECT does, or run_execute(s, execute)
for what changes something, as a write does; a Scheduled run's Run cell,
built.run(query, execute), picks for each of its Statements the same way. You edit nothing
here, unless your connection names its two calls another way: then change the calls query and
execute make below, and the names connect checks for, ("query", "execute"), to yours. Let your
connection's two calls share one session, so a Session setting sent through execute holds for
what is sent after it; and let each raise when the warehouse reports a failure, since the
Toolbox knows a step failed only when your connection raises. Keep it importing nothing from
references/, building_blocks/ or tables/, so any notebook or script of yours can import it.
"""

from typing import NoReturn

import pandas as pd

# The connection this notebook gave connect(...): None until it connects.
_connection = None


def connect(connection: object) -> None:
    """Set the connection query and execute pass each Hive string to, such as connect(conn),
    where conn is your team's connection: an object with a query(sql) and an execute(sql)."""
    global _connection
    missing = [call for call in ("query", "execute")
               if not callable(getattr(connection, call, None))]
    if missing:
        calls = " or ".join(f"{call}(sql)" for call in missing)
        _refuse(
            what=f"The connection given to connect(...), a {type(connection).__name__}, "
            f"has no {calls} to call.",
            why="query passes each Hive string that gives back rows to your connection's "
            "query(sql), and execute each one that changes something to its execute(sql), "
            "so your connection needs both.",
            fix="Pass connect(...) the connection itself, the object with both, such as "
            "connect(conn), where conn is your team's connection. If yours names them another "
            "way, change the functions in your Project's warehouse.py: the names connect "
            "checks for, (\"query\", \"execute\"), and the calls query and execute make.",
            error=TypeError,
        )
    _connection = connection


def query(hive: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Run a Hive string that gives back rows, and give them back as a pandas DataFrame."""
    _check_connected("query")
    return _connection.query(hive)


def execute(hive: str) -> None:
    """Run a Hive string that changes something, such as a write, and give back nothing."""
    _check_connected("execute")
    _connection.execute(hive)


def _check_connected(call: str) -> None:
    """Refuse a call to query or execute made before this notebook connected."""
    if _connection is None:
        _refuse(
            what=f"No connection: call connect(...) first. {call}(...) was called before this "
            "notebook gave warehouse.py its connection.",
            why="Each notebook runs on its own, in a Python of its own, so each connects at "
            "its top: a connection given in another notebook, or before this one's kernel "
            "restarted, isn't this one's.",
            fix="In this notebook's setup cell, at its top, fill in the line connect(...) "
            "under its from warehouse import line with your connection, such as connect(conn), "
            "where conn is your team's connection, then run that cell again.",
            error=RuntimeError,
        )


def _refuse(what: str, why: str, fix: str, error: type[Exception]) -> NoReturn:
    """Stop, with the four parts every refusal of the Toolbox has."""
    raise error(f"\n  What happened:  {what}"
                f"\n  Why it matters: {why}"
                f"\n  Usual fix:      {fix}"
                "\n  Opt-out:        none - this one can't be switched off.")

The intermediate Example project

The intermediate Example project

A Project made for the Example database's tables ops.job_events, ops.job_owners, ops.region_costs, ops.region_budgets and ops.team_notes, laid out as yours is. It builds on the starter Example project: read that one first.

Its tables are of every kind: ops.job_events and ops.job_owners are partitioned by dt, the day, ops.region_costs by region and then dt, ops.region_budgets by region alone, and ops.team_notes not at all, with no Key. Its Saved tables are too.

It builds six Saved tables, each in a Table folder of its own, five made from the saved_table Template and one from quality_checks:

  • mart.job_day_costs: each job's cost per day, all regions added up, from ops.region_costs, which writes its days like 20260924. An incremental load: each run writes its last 3 days again.
  • mart.job_day_facts: each job's events, runs and minutes per day, beside the team that owned the job that day, looked up in ops.job_owners' snapshots: an as-of lookup, also writing its last 3 days.
  • mart.team_days: each team's day, read from the two tables above, so it is written after them, and week_totals, a weekly rollup that adds the saved days up into weeks.
  • mart.job_events_checks, from quality_checks: the row count of each dt of ops.job_events, and checks for repeated keys and NULLs.
  • mart.budget_overruns: each job's budget in its region, and the days its cost went over, partitioned by region, with a write per region.
  • mart.job_notes: each team note beside the jobs the team owned on the day it was written, not partitioned and with no Key, so each write replaces the whole table.

And a seventh, mart.team_failures, each team's events and fails per day, is a finished Conversion: Hive the ops team wrote, which writes ops_reports.team_failures, converted with project.convert("team_failures", ..., rename={"ops_reports.team_failures": "team_failures"}) into this Project's Dev Database, mart, as admin/databases.py names it. Its one # CONVERSION: mark was decided by keeping the opt-out, each Decision made in its Table reference, its location and its Key, and the table compared with its original, project.compare_batch("team_failures", query=query), which finished the batch: its folder is in admin/workflows/convert/finished/team_failures/. Its Table folder's conversion/ keeps the statement it came from, and its notebook the cells that compare the two.

And an eighth, mart.job_hours, the hours each job's runs took while each team owned it, is a finished Conversion of an Excel workbook, admin/workflows/convert/job_hours.xlsx, as how-to "Convert an Excel workbook" makes it: a Table of ops.job_events' rows with a formula, hours, a Power Query keeping each run's finish event and joining it to ops.job_owners for the team that owned the job that day, and a PivotTable over the query's Table, the hours of each job, with a slicer keeping the data team. It was converted with project.convert("job_hours", files=["job_hours.xlsx"], query=query, database="ops"), the Workbook check matching each of the PivotTable's cells, and its Decisions answered in the convert Workflow's decisions cell: its name, "name": "job_hours" in place of the proposed teams_pivot, which moved the files convert first wrote to admin/removed/; its location; and its Pivot filter, the slicer on team, "pivot_filters": {"team": "fold"}, so the table keeps every team, a row for each job and team, where the PivotTable showed the data team's. Its proposed Key, ["job_id", "team"], was accepted, and its compare against its query finished the batch: its folder is in admin/workflows/convert/finished/job_hours/, which keeps its Hive in scripts/job_hours.hql, renamed with the table.

And a Scheduled run, weekly_team, a notebook a scheduler runs top to bottom each week: it adds up a week of mart.team_days with week_totals, and its build writes the three tables that week reads first, mart.job_day_costs and mart.job_day_facts, then mart.team_days. Its parameter WEEK, a day of the week to add up, is passed in as text, such as "2026-09-23"; left as None, a Run value of the run's own, week_first_day, takes the week of the latest day ops.job_events holds.

Rows can come in late here, a bill a day or two after its day, so the three tables that count or add up rows per day write their last 3 days again on each run, and a day written again replaces itself. The number 3 is written once, in building_blocks/days_rewritten.py, which those tables read. mart.job_events_checks checks only the dt its READS names: a check is of the rows in on the day it runs, and a check written again later would hide what was missing then.

This README was written by hand, as an Example project's own note: a Project of yours needs none.

ops.region_costs is partitioned by region, then dt, which add_references read from the table into references/ops__region_costs.py, and writes its days like 20260924: building_blocks/costs_per_job.py writes them like 2026-09-24, as the project's other tables do.

admin/check_project.ipynb

Check the Project

Reports, and changes nothing: a Table folder, Table reference, Building block or Scheduled run no Project action made (one made by hand), and one the registry lists that isn't there, and the next step of each Workflow you have started, in workflows/. project.do_next() runs the steps it can for you. Adopt one made by hand with project.register(...), given its table's name, or the Building block's or Scheduled run's.

Code cell 1

import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parent))  # the Project: one up from admin/

from sqlglot_composer import project

It shows nothing.

  admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb, team_failures: every table the batch wrote passed its compare, and its folder is admin/workflows/convert/finished/team_failures/.

admin/check_references.ipynb

Check the Table references

Compares every Table reference in references/ with its table in the warehouse, through your query, and prints what differs, with the line to change. Then it checks the Key of each table that has one: that no two rows share it. It changes nothing in the warehouse; its first cell refreshes the Reference sheet, so it writes references/reference_sheet.csv and the Database modules again and records the Project's Lineage state, and no other file. A Saved table you haven't created yet reports that DESCRIBE failed, as each of the Example projects' does: that is expected. It reads each table from the Reference sheet: its columns, their types, its partition columns and its Key. A partitioned table's Key is checked on its latest Partition, the last SHOW PARTITIONS lists, since checking every one would read the whole table; the last cell checks it on another you name. A Saved table's storage, its folder and file format, isn't in the sheet: the last cell checks it on the table's own Table reference, and the check prints the lines to write there.

Code cell 1

import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parent))  # the Project: one up from admin/

from sqlglot_composer import project

It shows nothing.

Code cell 2

import csv

from sqlglot_composer import Table, all_of, check_key, check_table_reference, equals
from warehouse import connect, query

# Your connection to the warehouse. Each notebook runs on its own, so each connects here, at
# its top, before it calls query or execute: write yours in the line below and delete its #,
# such as connect(your_library.connect(...)). A connection kept in a file outside the Project,
# such as team_connection.py, is imported by these lines, written in that line's place:
#     sys.path.append("C:/work/connections")  # the folder team_connection.py is in
#     from team_connection import conn
#     connect(conn)
connect(conn)  # conn: your team's connection; here, the Example database's

# The Reference sheet: a row per column of every Table reference in references/, with its
# type, and "yes" for a partition column and for a column in the Key.
print(project.refresh_reference_sheet())
sheet = pathlib.Path.cwd().parent / "references" / "reference_sheet.csv"
with open(sheet, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as file:
    rows = list(csv.DictReader(file))

It prints

Wrote references/reference_sheet.csv: 53 rows, 13 tables.

Code cell 3

# Each table, as the Reference sheet holds it, compared with the table in the warehouse;
# then its Key, where it has one.
for name in sorted({row["table"] for row in rows}):
    columns = [row for row in rows if row["table"] == name]
    partitions = [row["column"] for row in columns if row["partition"] == "yes"]
    key = [row["column"] for row in columns if row["in_key"] == "yes"]
    table = Table(name, columns={row["column"]: row["type"] or None for row in columns},
                  partitioned_by=partitions, key=key or None)
    found = check_table_reference(table, query=query)
    print(found)
    database, _, short = name.rpartition(".")
    if not key:
        print(f"{name} has no Key, so there is no Key to check.")
    elif not found.ok:
        print(f"{name}: its Key is checked once the problems above are fixed.")
    elif partitions:
        # Its Key on its latest Partition, of those SHOW PARTITIONS lists, such as
        # dt=2026-09-24: checking every one would read the whole table.
        shown = query(f"SHOW PARTITIONS {name}")
        partition_names = sorted(str(each) for each in shown.iloc[:, 0])
        if not partition_names:
            print(f"{name} holds no Partition yet, so there are no rows to check its Key on.")
        else:
            print(f"{name} is partitioned by {' and '.join(partitions)}, so its Key is checked "
                  f"on its latest Partition, the last of {len(partition_names)} that SHOW "
                  "PARTITIONS lists: checking every one would read the whole table.")
            on_the_latest = []
            for pair in partition_names[-1].split("/"):
                column, value = pair.split("=", 1)
                on_the_latest.append(equals(getattr(table, column), value))
            print(check_key(table, query=query, where=all_of(*on_the_latest)))
        if len(partition_names) > 1:
            column, value = partition_names[0].split("/")[0].split("=", 1)
            print("To check it on another, in the last cell:\n"
                  f"    from references.{database}__{short} import {short}\n"
                  f"    print(check_key({short}, query=query, "
                  f'where=equals({short}.{column}, "{value}")))')
    else:
        print(check_key(table, query=query))
    print()

admin/databases.py

Show its 13 lines
"""databases - the settings of the two Databases you own: DEV, where
you build and check tables, and PROD, where tables other people rely on are written. Set each
once; the Workflows read them.

"database" is the Database's name, such as "mart_dev". "location_root" is the folder its
tables' files go under: a table with no folder of its own goes in
<location_root>/<database>.db/<table>, such as /data/your_team/dev/mart_dev.db/daily_runs.
Any other setting is yours, such as "run_as", the account your connection runs as: give it
in both, each its own value, and database_settings("mart_dev") gives DEV's to your code.
"""

DEV = {"database": "mart", "location_root": "<DEV_LOCATION_ROOT>"}
PROD = {"database": "<PROD_DATABASE>", "location_root": "<PROD_LOCATION_ROOT>"}

admin/lineage.ipynb

The Lineage state and the Lineage difference

admin/lineage/lineage_state.csv, the Lineage state, records the logic of each column of your Project: its own logic and the rows that count for it, and its Effective logic, the same traced through every Saved table it reads, down to the tables the Project doesn't write. A row is added each time one changes, saying when, at which commit and from which call. Each Project action that changes a table or a Table reference records it already, and so does each Table notebook's cell 5, export_hive(): run the record cell below, project.record_lineage_state(), after a change made any other way, such as a Table reference edited by hand. project.check() says when the state is behind.

The difference cell after it writes the Lineage difference, admin/lineage/<time>-difference.html, and its Markdown twin: each column with an Own change, its own logic changed, and each with an Inherited change, its own logic the same but something it reads, up its chain, changed, with that chain down to the change it comes from, and when admin/sent.csv shows each changed table was next written. SINCE takes a time, such as "2026-11-20", or a commit, such as "1a2b3c4": left None, it compares the newest recording with the one before. TABLES takes a table's name, such as "mart.team_days", or a list of them, to show only those and the chains that reach them. Neither cell reaches the warehouse, so this notebook needs no connect(...).

import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parent))  # the Project: one up from admin/

from sqlglot_composer import project

project.record_lineage_state()  # after a change no Project action or cell 5 made

It gives

Lineage state: no change since the last recording.

Code cell 3

SINCE = None  # a time or a commit, such as "2026-11-20"; None: the one before
TABLES = None  # a table's name or a list, such as "mart.team_days"; None: all
project.export_lineage_difference(since=SINCE, tables=TABLES)

It gives

Wrote admin/lineage/20260925-060000-difference.html and its Markdown twin, 20260925-060000-difference.md: 3 tables changed, since the recording of 2026-09-25 06:00:10, to the newest, of 2026-09-25 07:00:00: 3 columns (1 own, 2 inherited).

admin/new_block.ipynb

New Building block

Writes a Building block, a piece several Table notebooks share, into building_blocks/<name>.py, from templates/building_block.py, and registers it. Its name is a Python name, such as runs_per_job, and a notebook's logic cell imports it by that name: from building_blocks.runs_per_job import runs_per_job. Refuses a name already taken. Never touches the warehouse. Name each in BLOCKS, run the cells, then fill in each new file's <...> and TODO lines. remove.ipynb archives one once no notebook imports it.

Code cell 1

import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parent))  # the Project: one up from admin/

from sqlglot_composer import project

It shows nothing.

Code cell 2

BLOCKS = []  # each Building block to make, by its Python name, such as ["runs_per_job"]

It shows nothing.

Code cell 3

if BLOCKS:
    print(project.new_block(BLOCKS))

It shows nothing.

admin/refresh_reference_sheet.ipynb

Refresh the Reference sheet

Rebuilds references/reference_sheet.csv from every file in references/: one row per column, with its type, whether it is a partition column or in the Key, its Aliases and its description. A Table notebook's last cell refreshes only its own table's rows. It also writes each Database's file again, such as references/ops.py, so that from references import ops gives ops.jobs: run it once a Table reference is filled in.

Code cell 1

import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parent))  # the Project: one up from admin/

from sqlglot_composer import project

It shows nothing.

Code cell 2

project.refresh_reference_sheet()

It gives

Wrote references/reference_sheet.csv: 53 rows, 13 tables.

admin/remove.ipynb

Remove

Moves Table folders, Table references, Building blocks, Conversion folders and Scheduled runs into admin/removed/, each named by the time, and logs it. Nothing is deleted, and a table stays in the warehouse: drop it with admin.drop_table in workflows/change_a_saved_table/. Name what to archive in the cell below, then run the rest: each list left empty is passed over.

Code cell 1

import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parent))  # the Project: one up from admin/

from sqlglot_composer import project

It shows nothing.

Code cell 2

TABLES = []  # Table folders to archive, such as ["mart.daily_job_runs"]
REFERENCES = []  # Table references to archive, by their tables' names
BLOCKS = []  # Building blocks to archive, such as ["runs_per_job"]
CONVERSIONS = []  # Conversion folders to archive, such as ["weekly"]
SCHEDULED_RUNS = []  # Scheduled runs to archive, such as ["weekly"]

It shows nothing.

Code cell 3

if TABLES:
    print(project.remove_table(TABLES))

It shows nothing.

Code cell 4

if REFERENCES:
    print(project.remove_reference(REFERENCES))

It shows nothing.

Code cell 5

if BLOCKS:
    print(project.remove_block(BLOCKS))

It shows nothing.

Code cell 6

if CONVERSIONS:
    print(project.remove_conversion(CONVERSIONS))

It shows nothing.

Code cell 7

if SCHEDULED_RUNS:
    print(project.remove_scheduled_run(SCHEDULED_RUNS))

It shows nothing.

"""mart.teams_pivot - TODO: say in one line what one row is."""
from sqlglot_composer import Table

teams_pivot = Table(
    "mart.teams_pivot",
    columns={
        "job_id": "bigint",
        "sum_of_hours": "double",
    },
    key=["job_id"],  # proposed from its query; if it is wrong, answer "key" in the decisions cell
    location="/data/mart.db/job_hours",
    does_not_add_up=[],  # TODO: columns that are averages, ratios or distinct counts
)

# The name of this table, proposed for its final in the workbook. Answer "name" in the
# decisions cell: "proposed" keeps it; a name of your own renames this table, and its
# Total Row's table, if it has one, with it.
NAME = "job_hours"  # for PivotTable1 of job_hours.xlsx

# The database table each Table or range of the workbook it reads samples, proposed from
# its name, which needn't be the one your warehouse gives it. Answer "sources" in the
# decisions cell with the table it samples, in any database, such as {"ops.table1":
# "ops.job_runs"}, or {"ops.table1": "proposed"} to keep it.
SOURCES = {
    "ops.job_events": "ops.job_events",  # the database table job_events of job_hours.xlsx samples
    "ops.job_owners": "ops.job_owners",  # the database table job_owners of job_hours.xlsx samples
}

# What the PivotTable this table was made from leaves out: each Pivot filter, by its
# field, a Decision. Answer "pivot_filters" in the decisions cell: "keep" keeps the
# selection as converted; "fold", "batch" or "tables" keeps every value instead, as its
# checklist says.
PIVOT_FILTERS = {
    "team": "fold",  # the slicer on team keeps data. "keep": 1 table, that selection; "fold": 1 table, every team; "batch": 1 table, a Partition per team; "tables": at least 2 tables, one per team the PivotTable's cache held (data and finance)
}

# Rebuilt or History: "rebuilt" if each write replaces the whole table; "history" if
# each write keeps the rows already there, adding rows or replacing some, such as one
# day's Partition, so the table needs its past rows loaded first, its Seed. Answer
# "kind" in the decisions cell: "proposed" accepts the kind proposed below.
KIND = "rebuilt"  # TODO: proposed rebuilt: it is a query alone, which each write writes whole

# --- Aliases ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Your own names for this table's columns and conditions, written by hand below.
# Import one beside the table: from references.mart__teams_pivot import teams_pivot, <alias>
#
# A column Alias is a shorter name for one column, for you: the Hive, and the DataFrame you
# get back, still call the column by its own name. To rename an output, use AS(...).
#     <alias> = teams_pivot.sum_of_hours
# A condition Alias names a condition on this table alone, for WHERE or
# count_rows(where=...). Add what it calls, such as equals, to the import at the top.
#     <alias> = equals(teams_pivot.sum_of_hours, <value>)

# Checklist: mart.teams_pivot

Written again each time its Decisions are written: don't tick or edit it. Answer each open Decision in the decisions cell of admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb.

## Open Decisions

- kind: proposed a Rebuilt table, as it is a query alone, which each write writes whole: accept it with "proposed", or answer "rebuilt" if each write replaces the whole table, "history" if it keeps the rows already there, in references/mart__teams_pivot.py

## Notes, which block nothing

- FROM(job_events, reads_all_partitions=True), in the logic cell, keeps what the original did, as its `# CONVERSION:` mark says until you delete it. Without reads_all_partitions=True the Toolbox refuses the logic, saying: FROM(job_events) reads ops.job_events, but filters none of its partition columns: dt. If you want to, change what made it needed, such as a WHERE on the partition column, then take the opt-out off; the compare step checks the rows either way.
- LEFT_JOIN(o, reads_all_partitions=True), in the logic cell, keeps what the original did, as its `# CONVERSION:` mark says until you delete it. Without reads_all_partitions=True the Toolbox refuses the logic, saying: LEFT_JOIN(o, ON=...) reads ops.job_owners, but filters none of its partition columns: dt. If you want to, change what made it needed, such as a WHERE on the partition column, then take the opt-out off; the compare step checks the rows either way.
- sum_of(run_hours.hours, adds_up=True), in the logic cell, keeps what the original did, as its `# CONVERSION:` mark says until you delete it. Without adds_up=True the Toolbox refuses the logic, saying: sum_of(run_hours.hours) adds up run_hours.hours, which is a division. If you want to, change what made it needed, such as a WHERE on the partition column, then take the opt-out off; the compare step checks the rows either way.
- project.convert proposed its Key, (job_id), from its query: if it is wrong, answer "key" in the decisions cell, such as "key": ["a", "b"], or "key": None when no columns name one row.
- If it is a History table, which each write adds to, its copy in dev starts with none of the past rows its original holds. So the convert Workflow's write step first loads them, its Seed, once, copied from its original: SEED, in its Table reference, picks which, such as {"months": 1}, the month up to the newest Partition. To load others, answer "seed" in the decisions cell, such as {"dt": {"from": "2026-01-01"}}. A table with no Partitions copies every row. A Rebuilt table has no Seed. The write step's report says what its Seed loaded.
- Its name, teams_pivot, is proposed: PivotTable1 is Excel's default name, so it is named for its sheet, Teams. It is a Decision: answer "name" in the decisions cell, "proposed" to keep it, or a name of your own, such as "runs_by_job", which renames the table, and its Total Row's table, if it has one, with it.
- From the workbook, marked (rule PF3, in Copilot file 24): PivotTable1: Pivot filter: slicer team, data kept: a Decision of its table. Next: check the values it gives are the ones you want, as the compare step shows them.
- Pivot filter team, a Decision. What mart.teams_pivot shows: sum_of_hours by job_id, only where the slicer on team keeps data, as the workbook was last saved. Its answers, each given as "pivot_filters": {"team": ...}: "keep", proposed: 1 table, that selection alone. "fold": like dragging team from Filters to Rows in the PivotTable: 1 table, every team, team a column of mart.teams_pivot and part of what one row is, a row for each job_id and team. "batch": 1 table written one team at a time, each run writing its own team's rows: mart.teams_pivot is partitioned on team, a Partition per team (at least 2 Partitions), and the team each run writes is the Script tag TEAM, answered under "tags" with the Run value proposed there. "tables": like Excel's Show Report Filter Pages: a table per team, named mart.teams_pivot_<value>, such as mart.teams_pivot_data: team: at least 2 tables (data and finance). A layer is each field the table is filtered on, and "at least" as these are the values the PivotTable's cache held when the workbook was saved: the warehouse may hold more. Which to pick: to tab through each team in a presentation, as with a slicer, "fold" keeps every team in one table to slice later; "batch" when each team is written on its own schedule; "tables" only for a handful; "keep" when one selection is all anyone needs. A handful of tables, such as 3, is manageable; a hundred is a reason to "fold" or "batch" instead.

admin/removed/20260925-060000-mart__teams_pivot/conversion/in_dev.hql

Show its 1 lines
WITH job_events AS (SELECT job_id, event_type, dt, minutes / 60 AS hours FROM ops.job_events), run_hours AS (SELECT e.job_id, o.team, e.hours FROM job_events AS e LEFT JOIN ops.job_owners AS o ON e.job_id = o.job_id AND e.dt = o.dt WHERE e.event_type = 'finish') INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.teams_pivot SELECT job_id, SUM(hours) AS sum_of_hours FROM run_hours WHERE (team = 'data') /* Pivot filter: slicer team */ GROUP BY job_id

# Conversion mart.teams_pivot

- 28 lines of Hive, one query reading ops.job_events and ops.job_owners.
- Found:
  - the query becomes your own Saved table's write, INSERT_OVERWRITE(...)
- Opt-out added, marked # CONVERSION: at its line: FROM(job_events, reads_all_partitions=True)
- Opt-out added, marked # CONVERSION: at its line: LEFT_JOIN(o, reads_all_partitions=True)
- Opt-out added, marked # CONVERSION: at its line: sum_of(run_hours.hours, adds_up=True)
- Its Hive and the original's: The Statement's Hive and the original are the same query, once both are written out the same way.

admin/removed/20260925-060000-mart__teams_pivot/conversion/statement.hql

Show its 28 lines
WITH job_events AS (
  SELECT
    job_id,
    event_type,
    dt,
    minutes / 60 AS hours
  FROM ops.job_events
), run_hours AS (
  SELECT
    e.job_id,
    o.team,
    e.hours
  FROM job_events AS e
  LEFT JOIN ops.job_owners AS o
    ON e.job_id = o.job_id AND e.dt = o.dt
  WHERE
    e.event_type = 'finish'
)
SELECT
  job_id,
  SUM(hours) AS sum_of_hours
FROM run_hours
WHERE
  (
    team = 'data'
  ) /* Pivot filter: slicer team */
GROUP BY
  job_id;

admin/removed/20260925-060000-mart__teams_pivot/mart__teams_pivot.ipynb

An archived copy, kept in admin/removed/ when its table was renamed: nothing reads or runs it, so its cells are shown unrun.

mart.teams_pivot

A Saved table made by the batch job_hours, with project.convert(...), from the statements in conversion/statement.hql, as its script wrote them. Its logic cell builds the same query from the Toolbox's own functions, with create(), preview() and write().

Terms

  • Saved table: a table in the warehouse that only your own code writes, its files in a folder of its own.
  • Logic cell: the code cell whose first line is # logic: the table's imports and functions alone.
  • Decision: one thing about this table that only you decide, such as its location or its Key; conversion/checklist.md lists each one still open.
  • Key: the columns whose values name exactly one row of the table.
  • Original: the table the converted script writes, in the warehouse already, which this table copies, or, for a table made from a query, that query. conversion/in_dev.hql holds the original's Hive, which the cells comparing it read.

Steps

1. Answer its Decisions. Goal: each Decision made. Action: answer each open Decision conversion/checklist.md lists in the decisions cell of admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb. Done when conversion/checklist.md says None under Open Decisions, once that Workflow's next step writes your answers in; then step 2. 2. Run the cells. Goal: its rows, its Hive and its lineage, tried. Action: run the code cells top to bottom. Done when each runs with no error.

Decision rules

  • If you still want to compare it with the original, keep the cells comparing them.
  • If you don't, delete those cells: they stay until you delete them.

Self-check

  • conversion/checklist.md says None under Open Decisions.
  • Each code cell runs top to bottom with no error.

Code cell 2

# logic
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS,
    Clause,
    FROM,
    GROUP_BY,
    LEFT_JOIN,
    SELECT,
    Statement,
    WHERE,
    all_of,
    derived,
    equals,
    statement,
    sum_of,
)
from references import ops
from sqlglot_composer import create_table  # for create()
from sqlglot_composer import INSERT_OVERWRITE, Statement, statement  # for write()
from references.mart__teams_pivot import teams_pivot  # the Saved table's own Table reference

job_events_2 = ops.job_events
job_events = derived("job_events", statement(
    SELECT(
        job_events_2.job_id,
        job_events_2.event_type,
        job_events_2.dt,
        AS(job_events_2.minutes / 60, "hours"),
    ),
    # CONVERSION: reads_all_partitions=True, as the original did; check it
    FROM(job_events_2, reads_all_partitions=True),
))
e = AS(job_events, "e")
o = AS(ops.job_owners, "o")
run_hours = derived("run_hours", statement(
    SELECT(
        e.job_id,
        o.team,
        e.hours,
    ),
    FROM(e),
    # CONVERSION: reads_all_partitions=True, as the original did; check it
    LEFT_JOIN(o, ON=all_of(
        equals(e.job_id, o.job_id),
        equals(e.dt, o.dt),
    ), reads_all_partitions=True),
    WHERE(equals(e.event_type, "finish")),
))


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses of the query, which converted() and a write share."""
    return [
        SELECT(
            run_hours.job_id,
            # CONVERSION: adds_up=True, as the original did; check it
            AS(sum_of(run_hours.hours, adds_up=True), "sum_of_hours"),
        ),
        FROM(run_hours),
        WHERE(equals(run_hours.team, "data")),
        GROUP_BY(run_hours.job_id),
    ]


def converted() -> Statement:
    """The query, as the Hive this Conversion was made from writes it."""
    return statement(*rows())


def create() -> Statement:
    """Create mart.teams_pivot from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(teams_pivot, may_exist=True)


def preview() -> Statement:
    """The rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return converted()


def write() -> Statement:
    """Save the rows into mart.teams_pivot, as the original's INSERT did."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(teams_pivot), *rows())

admin/run_tables.ipynb

Run the tables

Writes every table: each Table notebook's write(), read from its logic cell with project.load, each before the tables that read it. First, with no execute, it prints each Hive in that order and sends nothing; then, with your query and execute, it sends them. tables= picks some; leave it out for every Table folder the registry lists. The cell under the Project's path connects to the warehouse, as every notebook that reaches it does: a Run value that reads the warehouse needs it even to print.

-- 1 of 9: mart.job_day_costs
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
WITH costs_per_job AS (
  SELECT
    region_costs.job_id,
    SUM(region_costs.cost_cents) AS cost_cents,
    CONCAT(
      SUBSTRING(region_costs.dt, 1, 4),
      '-',
      SUBSTRING(region_costs.dt, 5, 2),
      '-',
      SUBSTRING(region_costs.dt, 7, 2)
    ) AS dt
  FROM ops.region_costs AS region_costs
  WHERE
    region_costs.dt BETWEEN '20260922' AND '20260924'
  GROUP BY
    region_costs.job_id,
    CONCAT(
      SUBSTRING(region_costs.dt, 1, 4),
      '-',
      SUBSTRING(region_costs.dt, 5, 2),
      '-',
      SUBSTRING(region_costs.dt, 7, 2)
    )
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.job_day_costs PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  costs_per_job.job_id,
  costs_per_job.cost_cents,
  costs_per_job.dt
FROM costs_per_job;
-- 2 of 9: mart.job_day_facts
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
WITH events_per_job AS (
  SELECT
    job_events.job_id,
    job_events.dt,
    COUNT(*) AS events,
    COUNT(CASE WHEN job_events.event_type IN ('start', 'restart') THEN 1 END) AS runs,
    COALESCE(
      SUM(
        CASE WHEN job_events.event_type IN ('finish', 'fail') THEN job_events.minutes END
      ),
      0
    ) AS minutes
  FROM ops.job_events AS job_events
  WHERE
    job_events.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-22' AND '2026-09-24'
  GROUP BY
    job_events.job_id,
    job_events.dt
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.job_day_facts PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  events_per_job.job_id,
  job_owners.team,
  events_per_job.events,
  events_per_job.runs,
  events_per_job.minutes,
  events_per_job.dt
FROM events_per_job
LEFT JOIN ops.job_owners AS job_owners
  ON job_owners.job_id = events_per_job.job_id
  AND job_owners.dt = events_per_job.dt
  AND job_owners.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-22' AND '2026-09-24';
-- 3 of 9: mart.team_days
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.team_days PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  job_day_facts.team,
  SUM(job_day_facts.events) AS events,
  SUM(job_day_facts.runs) AS runs,
  SUM(job_day_facts.minutes) AS minutes,
  SUM(job_day_costs.cost_cents) AS cost_cents,
  job_day_facts.dt
FROM mart.job_day_facts AS job_day_facts
LEFT JOIN mart.job_day_costs AS job_day_costs
  ON job_day_costs.job_id = job_day_facts.job_id
  AND job_day_costs.dt = job_day_facts.dt
  AND job_day_costs.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-22' AND '2026-09-24'
WHERE
  job_day_facts.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-22' AND '2026-09-24'
GROUP BY
  job_day_facts.dt,
  job_day_facts.team;
-- 4 of 9: mart.job_events_checks
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.job_events_checks PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  COUNT(*) AS row_count,
  job_events.dt
FROM ops.job_events AS job_events
WHERE
  job_events.dt = '2026-09-24'
GROUP BY
  job_events.dt;
-- 5 of 9: mart.budget_overruns
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.budget_overruns PARTITION(region = 'eu')
SELECT
  region_costs.job_id,
  region_budgets.budget_cents,
  COUNT(*) AS days,
  COUNT(CASE WHEN region_costs.cost_cents > region_budgets.budget_cents THEN 1 END) AS days_over
FROM ops.region_costs AS region_costs
JOIN ops.region_budgets AS region_budgets
  ON region_budgets.job_id = region_costs.job_id AND region_budgets.region = 'eu'
WHERE
  region_costs.region = 'eu' AND region_costs.dt BETWEEN '20260911' AND '20260924'
GROUP BY
  region_costs.job_id,
  region_budgets.budget_cents;
-- 6 of 9: mart.budget_overruns
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.budget_overruns PARTITION(region = 'us')
SELECT
  region_costs.job_id,
  region_budgets.budget_cents,
  COUNT(*) AS days,
  COUNT(CASE WHEN region_costs.cost_cents > region_budgets.budget_cents THEN 1 END) AS days_over
FROM ops.region_costs AS region_costs
JOIN ops.region_budgets AS region_budgets
  ON region_budgets.job_id = region_costs.job_id AND region_budgets.region = 'us'
WHERE
  region_costs.region = 'us' AND region_costs.dt BETWEEN '20260911' AND '20260924'
GROUP BY
  region_costs.job_id,
  region_budgets.budget_cents;
-- 7 of 9: mart.job_notes
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.job_notes
SELECT
  job_owners.job_id,
  team_notes.team,
  team_notes.note,
  team_notes.written_on
FROM ops.team_notes AS team_notes
JOIN ops.job_owners AS job_owners
  ON job_owners.team = team_notes.team
  AND job_owners.dt = team_notes.written_on
  AND job_owners.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-11' AND '2026-09-24';
-- 8 of 9: mart.team_failures
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
WITH ev AS (
  SELECT
    e.job_id,
    e.dt,
    e.event_type
  FROM ops.job_events AS e
  WHERE
    e.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-20' AND '2026-09-24'
    AND e.event_type IN ('start', 'retry', 'fail', 'finish')
), per_team AS (
  SELECT
    o.team,
    ev.dt,
    COUNT(*) AS events,
    SUM(CASE WHEN ev.event_type = 'fail' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS fails
  FROM ev
  JOIN ops.job_owners AS o
    ON o.job_id = ev.job_id AND o.dt = ev.dt
  GROUP BY
    o.team,
    ev.dt
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.team_failures PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  p.team,
  CASE p.team WHEN 'data' THEN 'Data platform' WHEN 'web' THEN 'Web' ELSE 'Other' END AS team_name,
  p.events,
  p.fails,
  p.dt
FROM per_team AS p;
-- 9 of 9: mart.job_hours
WITH job_events AS (
  SELECT
    job_events.job_id,
    job_events.event_type,
    job_events.dt,
    job_events.minutes / 60 AS hours
  FROM ops.job_events AS job_events
), run_hours AS (
  SELECT
    e.job_id,
    o.team,
    e.hours
  FROM job_events AS e
  LEFT JOIN ops.job_owners AS o
    ON e.job_id = o.job_id AND e.dt = o.dt
  WHERE
    e.event_type = 'finish'
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.job_hours
SELECT
  run_hours.job_id,
  run_hours.team,
  SUM(run_hours.hours) AS sum_of_hours
FROM run_hours
GROUP BY
  run_hours.job_id,
  run_hours.team;

admin/scheduled_runs.ipynb

Scheduled runs, by hand

What no Scheduled run does each time it runs, since it would be work for nothing each time: run these cells by hand, when you want them. To make a Scheduled run, use the schedule Workflow, workflows/schedule/schedule.ipynb. project.check() reports a loop among your tables, two or more each waiting on another so that none can be written first, naming the logic cell to mend, and changes nothing. project.export_project_lineage() writes lineage/<time>-project.html and its Markdown twin: every Saved table, what reads it, and which Scheduled runs write it, a table more than one writes marked and a loop drawn red. It builds each Scheduled run as its Build cell does, with its parameters' defaults, and sends nothing. Name Scheduled runs in RUNS for each one's own: project.export_run_lineage(RUNS) writes its lineage into its folder's lineage/, and project.export_run_hive(RUNS) its built Hive, in order, into <name>.hql beside its notebook, to read, never to edit. Name tables in TABLES for project.describe_logic(TABLES): each logic function of their Table notebooks, its hints and its docstring's first sentence, to paste under Copilot file 18. Each list left empty is passed over. The cell under the Project's path connects to the warehouse: a Run value that reads the warehouse needs it even to build.

admin/templates/building_block.py

Show its 56 lines
"""<BLOCK> - TODO: say in one line what one row of it is.

Fill in every <...> in the code below this text, brackets and all: Python stops at one left
in. They are:
    <SOURCE_NAME> and <SOURCE_TABLE>: the table it reads, by its file in references/ and its
        name there, such as ops__job_runs and job_runs.
    <GROUP_COLUMN>: the column each row is for, such as job_id.
    <NUMBER_COLUMN>: a column of numbers to add up, such as duration_mins.
    <TOTAL_NAME>: the name of what it adds up to, in quotes, such as "minutes".

Why: TODO: which tables' notebooks read it, so they can never work it out two ways.

project.new_block("<BLOCK>") wrote this file from admin/templates/building_block.py. A
Building block is a piece several notebooks share; this one is a Derived table, a Statement
given a name, which each Statement reading it writes at its top, as WITH <BLOCK> AS (...). In a
notebook's logic cell, import it, and read it like a table, given the rows it reads, such
as READS, the condition at the top of a saved_table notebook's logic cell:

    from building_blocks.<BLOCK> import <BLOCK>


    def preview() -> Statement:
        per_group = <BLOCK>(READS)
        return statement(SELECT(per_group.<GROUP_COLUMN>, per_group.row_count),
                         FROM(per_group))
"""

from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, SELECT, WHERE, Condition, Table, count_rows, derived, statement,
    sum_of,
)
from references.<SOURCE_NAME> import <SOURCE_TABLE>  # the Table reference it reads


def <BLOCK>(reads: Condition) -> Table:  # named as its file is
    """One row per group of the rows `reads` picks: its rows, and its total.

    `reads` is a condition on the table it reads, such as equals(job_runs.dt, "2026-09-24").
    On a partitioned table, it must filter one of its partition columns.
    """
    return derived(
        "<BLOCK>",  # the name the Hive gives it, the same as its file's
        statement(
            SELECT(
                <SOURCE_TABLE>.<GROUP_COLUMN>,  # what each row is for
                # to keep each Partition's rows apart, add its partition column here and in
                # GROUP_BY, such as job_runs.dt
                AS(count_rows(), "row_count"),
                # add a line like the next for each number to add up
                AS(sum_of(<SOURCE_TABLE>.<NUMBER_COLUMN>), <TOTAL_NAME>),
            ),
            FROM(<SOURCE_TABLE>),
            WHERE(reads),
            GROUP_BY(<SOURCE_TABLE>.<GROUP_COLUMN>),
        ),
    )

# %% [markdown]
# # <DB>.<TABLE>
#
# Fill in every `<...>` in the code cells below, brackets and all: the notebook won't run with
# one left. They are:
#   - `<CHECKED_NAME>` and `<CHECKED>`: the table checked, by its file in references/ and
#     its name there, such as `ops__job_events` and `job_events`;
#   - `<READS>`: which of its rows are checked, as a condition on it, such as
#     `equals(job_runs.dt, "2026-09-24")` or `equals(region_costs.region, "eu")`. On a
#     partitioned table, it must filter one of its partition columns. A table that isn't
#     partitioned can be read whole: delete the line `READS = <READS>` and each line
#     `WHERE(READS),` instead;
#   - `<KEY_COLUMN>`: the column that picks out one row, such as `event_id`;
#   - `<PARTITION_COLUMN>`: the column of the table checked that fills this table's partition
#     column, such as `dt` or `region`. If this table isn't partitioned, delete the two lines
#     of rows() naming `<PARTITION_COLUMN>`, and write `PARTITIONED_BY = []`, instead.
#
# ## Terms
#
# - **Logic**: the code cell that starts `# logic`, cell 2. It only imports and defines, so
#   `project.load("<DB>.<TABLE>")` can run it from anywhere else, for lineage across your
#   tables and for `admin/run_tables.ipynb`, which sends its `write()`.
# - **Table reference**: `references/<NAME>.py`, the script describing this table: its
#   columns and their types, its Partitions and its Key.
# - **Partition**: the rows of a table that share one value of each column it is partitioned
#   by.
# - **Key**: the columns whose values name exactly one row of the table.
# - **Decision**: one thing about this table that only you decide, such as its location; its
#   Table reference says whether it is made.
#
# ## Steps
#
# 1. **Fill in each `<...>`.** Goal: logic that runs. Action: type over each one listed above,
#    in the code cells. Done when no code cell holds a `<...>`; then step 2.
# 2. **Save the notebook.** Goal: the logic its Table reference is written from. Action: save
#    it. Done when it is saved; then step 3.
# 3. **Write its Table reference.** Goal: a Table reference written from the logic. Action:
#    run `project.reference_from_logic("<DB>.<TABLE>")`, a new_table Workflow step, which
#    writes `references/<NAME>.py` from the logic, for you to answer.
#    It lists one column, "row_count", a count, "bigint", then the partition column counted,
#    typed as the table checked types it, as PARTITIONED_BY names it. Its Key, proposed from
#    the logic, is that partition column: one row per Partition counted.
#    Done when `references/<NAME>.py` lists those columns; then step 4.
# 4. **Answer its Decisions.** Goal: each Decision made. Action: answer each open Decision its
#    Table reference leaves, such as its location, in the new_table Workflow's decisions
#    cell. Done when its Table reference leaves no Decision open; then step 5.
# 5. **Say what one row is.** Goal: a reader knows what the table holds. Action: write one
#    line saying what one row of <DB>.<TABLE> is, in place of the TODO line below. Done when
#    no TODO line is left in this cell; then step 6.
# 6. **Run the cells.** Goal: the logic tried, its lineage and Hive written, and its rows in
#    the Reference sheet. Action: run the code cells top to bottom, once its Table reference
#    is written from the logic. Done when each runs with no error, and cell 3 shows the rows
#    `preview()` gives.
#
# ## This table
#
# TODO: say in one line what one row of <DB>.<TABLE> is.
#
# Quality checks on a table you read. The row count of each of its Partitions READS picks is
# saved, so you can read them side by side and see one that looks too small; and checks run on
# the same rows: repeated_keys looks for a key with more than one row, and null_counts counts
# NULLs.
#
# Made by `project.new_table("<DB>.<TABLE>", template="quality_checks")` from
# `admin/templates/quality_checks.py`, which filled in the database, the table and its file's name,
# <NAME>.
#
# ## The code cells
#
# - 1: the Project, your connection, connected with `connect(...)`, and your query.
# - 2: the logic, which starts `# logic`.
# - 3: try it.
# - 4: its lineage.
# - 5: its Hive, in <NAME>.hql.
# - 6: its rows in the Reference sheet.

# ## Self-check
#
# - No code cell holds a `<...>`.
# - `references/<NAME>.py` lists the columns `write()` saves, and leaves no Decision open.
# - One line above says what one row is, and no TODO line is left.

# %%
# 1. The Project and your query
import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parents[1]))  # the Project folder: two up from here

from sqlglot_composer import export_hive, export_lineage, project, run_query, show_hive
from warehouse import connect, query

# Your connection to the warehouse. Each notebook runs on its own, so each connects here, at
# its top, before it calls query or execute: write yours in the line below and delete its #,
# such as connect(your_library.connect(...)). A connection kept in a file outside the Project,
# such as team_connection.py, is imported by these lines, written in that line's place:
#     sys.path.append("C:/work/connections")  # the folder team_connection.py is in
#     from team_connection import conn
#     connect(conn)
# connect(your_connection)

# %%
# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the rows it checks, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, HAVING, INSERT_OVERWRITE, SELECT, WHERE, Clause, Column,
    Statement, all_columns, count_rows, create_table, equals, is_null, more_than, statement,
)
from references.<NAME> import <TABLE>  # this table's own Table reference, of row counts
# the table checked, such as: from references.ops__job_events import job_events
from references.<CHECKED_NAME> import <CHECKED>

# The rows checked: change it to check others. A condition that calls something other
# than equals, such as between or is_in, needs it added to the import above.
READS = <READS>

# This table's partition columns, in the order its SELECT gives them, last: [] for none.
PARTITIONED_BY = ["<PARTITION_COLUMN>"]


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create <DB>.<TABLE> from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(<TABLE>, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The row count of each Partition READS picks: the clauses preview and write share. The
    partition column comes last in SELECT, so INSERT_OVERWRITE writes each count into the
    Partition of the same value, replacing only those."""
    return [
        SELECT(
            AS(count_rows(), "row_count"),
            <CHECKED>.<PARTITION_COLUMN>,  # the Partition counted: this table's partition column
        ),
        FROM(<CHECKED>),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(<CHECKED>.<PARTITION_COLUMN>),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the row counts, replacing the Partitions they fill."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(<TABLE>), *rows())


def repeated_keys() -> Statement:
    """Each key that picks out more than one of the rows READS picks: it should find no
    rows."""
    return statement(
        SELECT(<CHECKED>.<KEY_COLUMN>, AS(count_rows(), "times_seen")),
        FROM(<CHECKED>),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(<CHECKED>.<KEY_COLUMN>),
        HAVING(more_than(count_rows(), 1)),  # tested once the rows of each key are counted
    )


def column_name(column: Column) -> str:
    """A column's own name: a column prints as its table and its name, such as
    job_events.minutes, and this keeps the part after the dot."""
    return str(column).split(".")[-1]


def null_counts() -> Statement:
    """How many of the rows READS picks hold NULL in each column, as one row: null_minutes
    counts the rows whose minutes is NULL, and so on."""
    counts = [AS(count_rows(where=is_null(column)), f"null_{column_name(column)}")
              for column in all_columns(<CHECKED>)]
    return statement(
        SELECT(*counts),
        FROM(<CHECKED>),
        WHERE(READS),
    )

# %%
# 3. Try it: the Hive of each step, then the rows
show_hive([create(), write()])
run_query(preview(), query)
run_query(repeated_keys(), query)  # should have no rows
run_query(null_counts(), query)

# %%
# 4. Lineage: a new pair in lineage/, named by the time and this table, beside the ones before
export_lineage(write())

# %%
# 5. Its Hive: <NAME>.hql beside this notebook, from the logic as last saved, then the
#    Project's Lineage state recorded: run it after each change to the logic
export_hive()

# %%
# 6. The Reference sheet: this table's rows, from references/<NAME>.py
project.refresh_reference_sheet("<DB>.<TABLE>")

# %%
# 1. The Project and your query
import pathlib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, str(pathlib.Path.cwd().parents[1]))  # the Project folder: two up from here

from sqlglot_composer import export_hive, export_lineage, project, run_query, show_hive
from warehouse import connect, query

# Your connection to the warehouse. Each notebook runs on its own, so each connects here, at
# its top, before it calls query or execute: write yours in the line below and delete its #,
# such as connect(your_library.connect(...)). A connection kept in a file outside the Project,
# such as team_connection.py, is imported by these lines, written in that line's place:
#     sys.path.append("C:/work/connections")  # the folder team_connection.py is in
#     from team_connection import conn
#     connect(conn)
# connect(your_connection)

# %%
# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the rows it reads, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, INSERT_OVERWRITE, SELECT, WHERE, Clause, Statement, count_rows,
    create_table, equals, statement,
)
from references.<NAME> import <TABLE>  # this table's own Table reference
# each table it reads, such as: from references.ops__job_runs import job_runs
from references.<SOURCE_NAME> import <SOURCE_TABLE>

# The rows write() reads: change it to write others. A condition that calls something other
# than equals, such as between or is_in, needs it added to the import above.
READS = <READS>

# This table's partition columns, in the order its SELECT gives them, last: [] for none.
PARTITIONED_BY = ["<PARTITION_COLUMN>"]


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create <DB>.<TABLE> from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(<TABLE>, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses preview and write share, so the two can never disagree: a count per group
    of the rows READS picks.

    A partitioned table's partition columns come last in SELECT, in its Table reference's
    order, named as it names them: INSERT_OVERWRITE fills each Partition from the rows' own
    values, and replaces only the Partitions they fill. They stay in GROUP_BY too, so each
    Partition's rows stay apart.
    """
    return [
        SELECT(
            <SOURCE_TABLE>.<GROUP_COLUMN>,  # what each row is for
            AS(count_rows(), <COUNT_NAME>),
            <SOURCE_TABLE>.<PARTITION_COLUMN>,  # this table's partition column, last
        ),
        FROM(<SOURCE_TABLE>),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(
            <SOURCE_TABLE>.<GROUP_COLUMN>,
            <SOURCE_TABLE>.<PARTITION_COLUMN>,  # kept, so each Partition's rows stay apart
        ),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the rows, replacing the Partitions they fill, or the whole table if it
    isn't partitioned: sent twice, it leaves the same rows, not twice as many."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(<TABLE>), *rows())

# %%
# 3. Try it: the Hive of each step, then the rows
show_hive([create(), write()])
run_query(preview(), query)

export_hive()

project.refresh_reference_sheet("<DB>.<TABLE>")
