# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the days it writes, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    FROM, INSERT_OVERWRITE, SELECT, Clause, Statement, create_table, statement,
)
from building_blocks.days_rewritten import first_day_written
from building_blocks.events_per_job import events_per_job
from building_blocks.owner_on_day import owner_on_day
from references.mart__job_day_facts import job_day_facts
from references.ops__job_owners import job_owners

DAY = "2026-09-24"  # the last day write() writes, the Example database's last day


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.job_day_facts from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(job_day_facts, may_exist=True)


def rows(first_day: str, last_day: str) -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses every step below shares: each job's day beside its team that day. The day
    comes last in SELECT: INSERT_OVERWRITE writes each day's rows into that day's Partition."""
    events = events_per_job(first_day, last_day)
    return [
        SELECT(events.job_id, job_owners.team, events.events, events.runs, events.minutes,
               events.dt),
        FROM(events),
        owner_on_day(events.job_id, events.dt, first_day=first_day, last_day=last_day),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows(first_day_written(DAY), DAY))


def write_days(first_day: str, last_day: str) -> Statement:
    """Every day from first_day to last_day, in one write; owner_on_day matches each day to
    that day's snapshot."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(job_day_facts), *rows(first_day, last_day))


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3, every run: the last 3 days, ending on DAY, in one write."""
    return write_days(first_day_written(DAY), DAY)

tables/mart__job_events_checks/mart__job_events_checks.ipynb

mart.job_events_checks

The row count of each dt of ops.job_events, saved every day, and checks of its rows.

Why: mart.job_day_facts counts ops.job_events' rows, so a day that came in short, or a key that came in twice, would give wrong numbers far from their cause. Saving each day's count lets you read the days side by side and see one that looks too small; the checks look for repeated keys and NULLs in the same rows.

Made by project.new_table("mart.job_events_checks", template="quality_checks") from admin/templates/quality_checks.py, then filled in.

The code cells: 1 the Project and your query, connected by connect(...); 2 the logic, which starts # logic; 3 try it; 4 its lineage; 5 its Hive, in mart__job_events_checks.hql; 6 its rows in the Reference sheet.

Code cell 2

# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the rows it checks, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, HAVING, INSERT_OVERWRITE, SELECT, WHERE, Clause, Column,
    Statement, all_columns, count_rows, create_table, equals, is_null, more_than, statement,
)
from references.mart__job_events_checks import job_events_checks
from references.ops__job_events import job_events

# The rows checked: the Example database's last dt. Change it to check others.
READS = equals(job_events.dt, "2026-09-24")


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.job_events_checks from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(job_events_checks, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The row count of each dt READS picks: the clauses preview and write share. dt comes
    last in SELECT: INSERT_OVERWRITE writes each count into the Partition of the same dt."""
    return [
        SELECT(AS(count_rows(), "row_count"), job_events.dt),
        FROM(job_events),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(job_events.dt),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the row write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the row counts, replacing the Partitions they fill."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(job_events_checks), *rows())


def repeated_keys() -> Statement:
    """Each event_id that picks out more than one of the rows READS picks: it should find no
    rows."""
    return statement(
        SELECT(job_events.event_id, AS(count_rows(), "times_seen")),
        FROM(job_events),
        WHERE(READS),
        GROUP_BY(job_events.event_id),
        HAVING(more_than(count_rows(), 1)),  # tested once the rows of each key are counted
    )


def column_name(column: Column) -> str:
    """A column's own name: a column prints as its table and its name, such as
    job_events.minutes, and this keeps the part after the dot."""
    return str(column).split(".")[-1]


def null_counts() -> Statement:
    """How many of the rows READS picks hold NULL in each column, as one row: null_minutes
    counts the rows whose minutes is NULL, and so on."""
    counts = [AS(count_rows(where=is_null(column)), f"null_{column_name(column)}")
              for column in all_columns(job_events)]
    return statement(
        SELECT(*counts),
        FROM(job_events),
        WHERE(READS),
    )

tables/mart__job_hours/conversion/checklist.md

Show its 18 lines
# Checklist: mart.job_hours

Written again each time its Decisions are written: don't tick or edit it. Answer each open Decision in the decisions cell of admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb.

## Open Decisions

- kind: proposed a Rebuilt table, as it is a query alone, which each write writes whole: accept it with "proposed", or answer "rebuilt" if each write replaces the whole table, "history" if it keeps the rows already there, in references/mart__job_hours.py

## Notes, which block nothing

- FROM(job_events, reads_all_partitions=True), in the logic cell, keeps what the original did, as its `# CONVERSION:` mark says until you delete it. Without reads_all_partitions=True the Toolbox refuses the logic, saying: FROM(job_events) reads ops.job_events, but filters none of its partition columns: dt. If you want to, change what made it needed, such as a WHERE on the partition column, then take the opt-out off; the compare step checks the rows either way.
- LEFT_JOIN(o, reads_all_partitions=True), in the logic cell, keeps what the original did, as its `# CONVERSION:` mark says until you delete it. Without reads_all_partitions=True the Toolbox refuses the logic, saying: LEFT_JOIN(o, ON=...) reads ops.job_owners, but filters none of its partition columns: dt. If you want to, change what made it needed, such as a WHERE on the partition column, then take the opt-out off; the compare step checks the rows either way.
- sum_of(run_hours.hours, adds_up=True), in the logic cell, keeps what the original did, as its `# CONVERSION:` mark says until you delete it. Without adds_up=True the Toolbox refuses the logic, saying: sum_of(run_hours.hours) adds up run_hours.hours, which is a division. If you want to, change what made it needed, such as a WHERE on the partition column, then take the opt-out off; the compare step checks the rows either way.
- project.convert proposed its Key, (job_id), from its query: if it is wrong, answer "key" in the decisions cell, such as "key": ["a", "b"], or "key": None when no columns name one row.
- If it is a History table, which each write adds to, its copy in dev starts with none of the past rows its original holds. So the convert Workflow's write step first loads them, its Seed, once, copied from its original: SEED, in its Table reference, picks which, such as {"months": 1}, the month up to the newest Partition. To load others, answer "seed" in the decisions cell, such as {"dt": {"from": "2026-01-01"}}. A table with no Partitions copies every row. A Rebuilt table has no Seed. The write step's report says what its Seed loaded.
- Its name, teams_pivot, is proposed: PivotTable1 is Excel's default name, so it is named for its sheet, Teams. It is a Decision: answer "name" in the decisions cell, "proposed" to keep it, or a name of your own, such as "runs_by_job", which renames the table, and its Total Row's table, if it has one, with it.
- From the workbook, marked (rule PF3, in Copilot file 24): PivotTable1: Pivot filter: slicer team, data kept: a Decision of its table. Next: check the values it gives are the ones you want, as the compare step shows them.
- Pivot filter team, a Decision. What mart.job_hours shows: sum_of_hours by job_id, only where the slicer on team keeps data, as the workbook was last saved. Its answers, each given as "pivot_filters": {"team": ...}: "keep", proposed: 1 table, that selection alone. "fold": like dragging team from Filters to Rows in the PivotTable: 1 table, every team, team a column of mart.job_hours and part of what one row is, a row for each job_id and team. "batch": 1 table written one team at a time, each run writing its own team's rows: mart.job_hours is partitioned on team, a Partition per team (at least 2 Partitions), and the team each run writes is the Script tag TEAM, answered under "tags" with the Run value proposed there. "tables": like Excel's Show Report Filter Pages: a table per team, named mart.job_hours_<value>, such as mart.job_hours_data: team: at least 2 tables (data and finance). A layer is each field the table is filtered on, and "at least" as these are the values the PivotTable's cache held when the workbook was saved: the warehouse may hold more. Which to pick: to tab through each team in a presentation, as with a slicer, "fold" keeps every team in one table to slice later; "batch" when each team is written on its own schedule; "tables" only for a handful; "keep" when one selection is all anyone needs. A handful of tables, such as 3, is manageable; a hundred is a reason to "fold" or "batch" instead.

tables/mart__job_hours/conversion/in_dev.hql

Show its 1 lines
WITH job_events AS (SELECT job_id, event_type, dt, minutes / 60 AS hours FROM ops.job_events), run_hours AS (SELECT e.job_id, o.team, e.hours FROM job_events AS e LEFT JOIN ops.job_owners AS o ON e.job_id = o.job_id AND e.dt = o.dt WHERE e.event_type = 'finish') INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.job_hours SELECT job_id, team, SUM(hours) AS sum_of_hours FROM run_hours GROUP BY job_id, team

tables/mart__job_hours/conversion/statement.hql

Show its 26 lines
WITH job_events AS (
  SELECT
    job_id,
    event_type,
    dt,
    minutes / 60 AS hours
  FROM ops.job_events
), run_hours AS (
  SELECT
    e.job_id,
    o.team,
    e.hours
  FROM job_events AS e
  LEFT JOIN ops.job_owners AS o
    ON e.job_id = o.job_id AND e.dt = o.dt
  WHERE
    e.event_type = 'finish'
)
SELECT
  job_id,
  team,
  SUM(hours) AS sum_of_hours
FROM run_hours
GROUP BY
  job_id,
  team;

-- The Hive of mart.job_hours, generated by export_hive() from the logic cell of

CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mart.job_hours (
  job_id BIGINT,
  team STRING,
  sum_of_hours DOUBLE,
  PRIMARY KEY (job_id, team) DISABLE NOVALIDATE

-- 2 of 2: write()
WITH job_events AS (
  SELECT
    job_events.job_id,
    job_events.event_type,
    job_events.dt,
    job_events.minutes / 60 AS hours
  FROM ops.job_events AS job_events
), run_hours AS (
  SELECT
    e.job_id,
    o.team,
    e.hours
  FROM job_events AS e
  LEFT JOIN ops.job_owners AS o
    ON e.job_id = o.job_id AND e.dt = o.dt
  WHERE
    e.event_type = 'finish'
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.job_hours
SELECT
  run_hours.job_id,
  run_hours.team,
  SUM(run_hours.hours) AS sum_of_hours
FROM run_hours
GROUP BY
  run_hours.job_id,
  run_hours.team;

tables/mart__job_hours/mart__job_hours.ipynb

mart.job_hours

A Saved table made by the batch job_hours, with project.convert(...), from the statements in conversion/statement.hql, as its script wrote them. Its logic cell builds the same query from the Toolbox's own functions, with create(), preview() and write().

Terms

  • Saved table: a table in the warehouse that only your own code writes, its files in a folder of its own.
  • Logic cell: the code cell whose first line is # logic: the table's imports and functions alone.
  • Decision: one thing about this table that only you decide, such as its location or its Key; conversion/checklist.md lists each one still open.
  • Key: the columns whose values name exactly one row of the table.
  • Original: the table the converted script writes, in the warehouse already, which this table copies, or, for a table made from a query, that query. conversion/in_dev.hql holds the original's Hive, which the cells comparing it read.

Steps

1. Answer its Decisions. Goal: each Decision made. Action: answer each open Decision conversion/checklist.md lists in the decisions cell of admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb. Done when conversion/checklist.md says None under Open Decisions, once that Workflow's next step writes your answers in; then step 2. 2. Run the cells. Goal: its rows, its Hive and its lineage, tried. Action: run the code cells top to bottom. Done when each runs with no error.

Decision rules

  • If you still want to compare it with the original, keep the cells comparing them.
  • If you don't, delete those cells: they stay until you delete them.

Self-check

  • conversion/checklist.md says None under Open Decisions.
  • Each code cell runs top to bottom with no error.

Code cell 2

# logic
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS,
    Clause,
    FROM,
    GROUP_BY,
    LEFT_JOIN,
    SELECT,
    Statement,
    WHERE,
    all_of,
    derived,
    equals,
    statement,
    sum_of,
)
from references import ops
from sqlglot_composer import create_table  # for create()
from sqlglot_composer import INSERT_OVERWRITE, Statement, statement  # for write()
from references.mart__job_hours import job_hours  # the Saved table's own Table reference

job_events_2 = ops.job_events
job_events = derived("job_events", statement(
    SELECT(
        job_events_2.job_id,
        job_events_2.event_type,
        job_events_2.dt,
        AS(job_events_2.minutes / 60, "hours"),
    ),
    FROM(job_events_2, reads_all_partitions=True),
))
e = AS(job_events, "e")
o = AS(ops.job_owners, "o")
run_hours = derived("run_hours", statement(
    SELECT(
        e.job_id,
        o.team,
        e.hours,
    ),
    FROM(e),
    LEFT_JOIN(o, ON=all_of(
        equals(e.job_id, o.job_id),
        equals(e.dt, o.dt),
    ), reads_all_partitions=True),
    WHERE(equals(e.event_type, "finish")),
))


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses of the query, which converted() and a write share."""
    return [
        SELECT(
            run_hours.job_id,
            run_hours.team,
            AS(sum_of(run_hours.hours, adds_up=True), "sum_of_hours"),
        ),
        FROM(run_hours),
        GROUP_BY(run_hours.job_id, run_hours.team),
    ]


def converted() -> Statement:
    """The query, as the Hive this Conversion was made from writes it."""
    return statement(*rows())


def create() -> Statement:
    """Create mart.job_hours from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(job_hours, may_exist=True)


def preview() -> Statement:
    """The rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return converted()


def write() -> Statement:
    """Save the rows into mart.job_hours, as the original's INSERT did."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(job_hours), *rows())

tables/mart__job_notes/mart__job_notes.ipynb

mart.job_notes

Each note a team wrote, beside each job the team owned on the day it was written.

Why: a note names only its team, and a team's jobs change, so the jobs a note is about are the ones the team owned that day, which ops.job_owners' snapshots say.

Made by project.new_table("mart.job_notes") from admin/templates/saved_table.py, then filled in. It isn't partitioned and has no Key, so write() replaces the whole table, and nothing is checked against a Key it doesn't have. A note matches each job its team owned that day, several rows of ops.job_owners, which is what this table is for: the join says many_matches=True, so the Toolbox doesn't warn about it. ops.team_notes isn't partitioned, so it is read whole; ops.job_owners is, so the days FIRST_DAY to LAST_DAY are filtered in JOIN's ON=.

The code cells: 1 the Project and your query, connected by connect(...); 2 the logic, which starts # logic; 3 try it; 4 its lineage; 5 its Hive, in mart__job_notes.hql; 6 its rows in the Reference sheet.

# 1. The Project and your query
import pathlib
import sys

from warehouse import connect, query

# Your connection to the warehouse. Each notebook runs on its own, so each connects here, at
# its top, before it calls query or execute: write yours in the line below and delete its #,
# such as connect(your_library.connect(...)). A connection kept in a file outside the Project,
# such as team_connection.py, is imported by these lines, written in that line's place:
#     sys.path.append("C:/work/connections")  # the folder team_connection.py is in

Code cell 2

# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the days it looks up, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    FROM, INSERT_OVERWRITE, JOIN, SELECT, Clause, Statement, all_of, between, create_table,
    equals, statement,
)
from references.mart__job_notes import job_notes
from references.ops__job_owners import job_owners
from references.ops__team_notes import team_notes

FIRST_DAY = "2026-09-11"  # the days of ops.job_owners a note's day is looked up in
LAST_DAY = "2026-09-24"


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.job_notes from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(job_notes, may_exist=True)


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses preview and write share: each note, once for each job its team owned on the
    day it was written."""
    return [
        SELECT(job_owners.job_id, team_notes.team, team_notes.note, team_notes.written_on),
        FROM(team_notes),
        JOIN(job_owners,
             ON=all_of(equals(job_owners.team, team_notes.team),
                       equals(job_owners.dt, team_notes.written_on),
                       between(job_owners.dt, FIRST_DAY, LAST_DAY)),
             many_matches=True),  # a note is about every job its team owned that day
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows())


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3: save the rows, replacing the whole table: sent twice, it leaves the same rows,
    not twice as many."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(job_notes), *rows())

tables/mart__team_days/mart__team_days.hql

-- The Hive of mart.team_days, generated by export_hive() from the logic cell of
-- mart__team_days.ipynb, beside this file. Edit the logic there, not this file: each
-- export_hive() writes it again, from the notebook as last saved. It holds 2 steps, in
-- the order they are sent: create() and write().

-- 1 of 2: create()
CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mart.team_days (
  team STRING,
  events BIGINT,
  runs BIGINT,
  minutes BIGINT,
  cost_cents BIGINT,
  PRIMARY KEY (team, dt) DISABLE NOVALIDATE
)
PARTITIONED BY (
  dt STRING
)
STORED AS ORC
LOCATION '/data/your_team/mart.db/team_days';

-- 2 of 2: write()
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.team_days PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  job_day_facts.team,
  SUM(job_day_facts.events) AS events,
  SUM(job_day_facts.runs) AS runs,
  SUM(job_day_facts.minutes) AS minutes,
  SUM(job_day_costs.cost_cents) AS cost_cents,
  job_day_facts.dt
FROM mart.job_day_facts AS job_day_facts
LEFT JOIN mart.job_day_costs AS job_day_costs
  ON job_day_costs.job_id = job_day_facts.job_id
  AND job_day_costs.dt = job_day_facts.dt
  AND job_day_costs.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-22' AND '2026-09-24'
WHERE
  job_day_facts.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-22' AND '2026-09-24'
GROUP BY
  job_day_facts.dt,
  job_day_facts.team;

tables/mart__team_days/mart__team_days.ipynb

mart.team_days

Each team's events, runs, minutes and cost per day, saved on every run, and read a week at a time.

Why: once mart.job_day_costs and mart.job_day_facts have saved a day, each team's day comes from a few saved rows per job; and a week's numbers come from 7 saved days, not from every event and bill again.

Made by project.new_table("mart.team_days") from admin/templates/saved_table.py, then filled in, writing its last 3 days on each run, and with week_totals, a weekly rollup. It reads the two Saved tables through their Table references, so it is written after them: run_tables sends it last. A job's day with no row in mart.job_day_costs, since it wasn't billed, keeps its events through LEFT_JOIN, with a NULL cost. week_totals adds the saved days up per week; all of its numbers add up, so a week of them means something.

The Saved tables are in your warehouse, not in the Example database, so its preview can't run here. team_day_from_job_events(DAY) can: it groups the Building blocks' rows by team the same way, from ops.job_events and ops.job_owners themselves, without the costs. Its lineage, cell 4, draws all three tables, through the other two notebooks' logic.

The code cells: 1 the Project and your query, connected by connect(...); 2 the logic, which starts # logic; 3 try it; 4 its lineage; 5 its Hive, in mart__team_days.hql; 6 its rows in the Reference sheet.

Code cell 2

# logic
# 2. The logic: imports, the days it writes, and functions only
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS, FROM, GROUP_BY, INSERT_OVERWRITE, LEFT_JOIN, SELECT, WHERE, Clause, Statement,
    all_of, between, create_table, equals, statement, sum_of, week_start,
)
from building_blocks.days_rewritten import first_day_written
from building_blocks.events_per_job import events_per_job
from building_blocks.owner_on_day import owner_on_day
from references.mart__job_day_costs import job_day_costs
from references.mart__job_day_facts import job_day_facts
from references.mart__team_days import team_days
from references.ops__job_owners import job_owners

DAY = "2026-09-24"  # the last day write() writes, the Example database's last day


def create() -> Statement:
    """Step 1: create mart.team_days from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(team_days, may_exist=True)


def rows(first_day: str, last_day: str) -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses every step below shares: each team's numbers per day of job_day_facts, the
    table in FROM. The costs are joined on their day as well as their job, so each day's facts
    meet only that day's costs; their days go in LEFT_JOIN's ON=, since a WHERE on
    job_day_costs would drop the days LEFT_JOIN keeps. The day comes last in SELECT:
    INSERT_OVERWRITE writes each day's rows into that day's Partition."""
    return [
        SELECT(
            job_day_facts.team,
            AS(sum_of(job_day_facts.events), "events"),
            AS(sum_of(job_day_facts.runs), "runs"),
            AS(sum_of(job_day_facts.minutes), "minutes"),
            AS(sum_of(job_day_costs.cost_cents), "cost_cents"),
            job_day_facts.dt,
        ),
        FROM(job_day_facts),
        LEFT_JOIN(job_day_costs,
                  ON=all_of(equals(job_day_costs.job_id, job_day_facts.job_id),
                            equals(job_day_costs.dt, job_day_facts.dt),
                            between(job_day_costs.dt, first_day, last_day))),
        WHERE(between(job_day_facts.dt, first_day, last_day)),
        GROUP_BY(job_day_facts.dt, job_day_facts.team),
    ]


def preview() -> Statement:
    """Step 2: the rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return statement(*rows(first_day_written(DAY), DAY))


def write_days(first_day: str, last_day: str) -> Statement:
    """Every day from first_day to last_day, in one write."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(team_days), *rows(first_day, last_day))


def write() -> Statement:
    """Step 3, every run, once the two tables it reads have written the same days: the last 3
    days, ending on DAY, in one write."""
    return write_days(first_day_written(DAY), DAY)


def week_totals(first_day: str, last_day: str) -> Statement:
    """Each team's events, runs, minutes and cost per week, from the days this table saved.
    A week that first_day or last_day cuts through counts only its days in between."""
    return statement(
        SELECT(
            AS(week_start(team_days.dt), "week"),
            team_days.team,
            AS(sum_of(team_days.events), "events"),
            AS(sum_of(team_days.runs), "runs"),
            AS(sum_of(team_days.minutes), "minutes"),
            AS(sum_of(team_days.cost_cents), "cost_cents"),
        ),
        FROM(team_days),
        WHERE(between(team_days.dt, first_day, last_day)),
        # GROUP_BY takes the SELECT's name for the week, "week"; the Hive writes the
        # week_start(...) calculation out again there, as Hive needs it
        GROUP_BY("week", team_days.team),
    )


def team_day_from_job_events(day: str) -> Statement:
    """Each team's events, runs and minutes for a day, from ops.job_events and ops.job_owners
    themselves, through the Building blocks mart.job_day_facts saves: so it runs on the
    Example database. The costs are left out: ops.region_costs writes its days like 20260924,
    so it can't be joined on the day to ops.job_events."""
    events = events_per_job(day, day)
    return statement(
        SELECT(
            job_owners.team,
            AS(sum_of(events.events), "events"),
            AS(sum_of(events.runs), "runs"),
            AS(sum_of(events.minutes), "minutes"),
        ),
        FROM(events),
        owner_on_day(events.job_id, events.dt, first_day=day, last_day=day),
        GROUP_BY(job_owners.team),
    )

It shows nothing.

Code cell 3

# 3. Try it: the Hive of each step, then each team's day from ops.job_events
show_hive([create(), write(), week_totals("2026-09-21", DAY)])
run_query(team_day_from_job_events(DAY), query)

tables/mart__team_failures/conversion/checklist.md

Show its 13 lines
# Checklist: mart.team_failures

Written again each time its Decisions are written: don't tick or edit it. Answer each open Decision in the decisions cell of admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb.

## Open Decisions

None: each Decision in references/mart__team_failures.py is made.

## Notes, which block nothing

- JOIN(o, reads_all_partitions=True), in the logic cell, keeps what the original did, as its `# CONVERSION:` mark says until you delete it. Without reads_all_partitions=True the Toolbox refuses the logic, saying: JOIN(o, ON=...) reads ops.job_owners, but filters none of its partition columns: dt. If you want to, change what made it needed, such as a WHERE on the partition column, then take the opt-out off; the compare step checks the rows either way.
- project.convert proposed its Key, (team, dt), from its query: if it is wrong, answer "key" in the decisions cell, such as "key": ["a", "b"], or "key": None when no columns name one row.
- If it is a History table, which each write adds to, its copy in dev starts with none of the past rows its original holds. So the convert Workflow's write step first loads them, its Seed, once, copied from its original: SEED, in its Table reference, picks which, such as {"months": 1}, the month up to the newest Partition. To load others, answer "seed" in the decisions cell, such as {"dt": {"from": "2026-01-01"}}. A table with no Partitions copies every row. A Rebuilt table has no Seed. The write step's report says what its Seed loaded.

tables/mart__team_failures/conversion/in_dev.hql

Show its 1 lines
WITH ev AS (SELECT e.job_id, e.dt, e.event_type FROM ops.job_events AS e WHERE e.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-20' AND '2026-09-24' AND e.event_type IN ('start', 'retry', 'fail', 'finish')), per_team AS (SELECT o.team, ev.dt, COUNT(*) AS events, SUM(CASE WHEN ev.event_type = 'fail' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS fails FROM ev JOIN ops.job_owners AS o ON o.job_id = ev.job_id AND o.dt = ev.dt GROUP BY o.team, ev.dt) INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.team_failures PARTITION(dt) SELECT p.team, CASE p.team WHEN 'data' THEN 'Data platform' WHEN 'web' THEN 'Web' ELSE 'Other' END AS team_name, p.events, p.fails, p.dt FROM per_team AS p

# Conversion mart.team_failures

- 20 lines of Hive, one query reading ops.job_events and ops.job_owners.
- Found:
  - its INSERT OVERWRITE mart.team_failures PARTITION(dt) becomes your own Saved table's write, INSERT_OVERWRITE(...), with the same partitions
  - the IN list of 4 values is the constant EVENT_TYPE_VALUES
  - the CASE on p.team is the constant TEAM_NAME_BY_TEAM
- Opt-out added, marked # CONVERSION: at its line: JOIN(o, reads_all_partitions=True)

tables/mart__team_failures/conversion/statement.hql

Show its 20 lines
WITH ev AS (
  SELECT e.job_id, e.dt, e.event_type
  FROM ops.job_events e
  WHERE e.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-20' AND '2026-09-24'
    AND e.event_type IN ('start', 'retry', 'fail', 'finish')
), per_team AS (
  SELECT o.team, ev.dt,
         COUNT(*) AS events,
         SUM(CASE WHEN ev.event_type = 'fail' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS fails
  FROM ev
  JOIN ops.job_owners o ON o.job_id = ev.job_id AND o.dt = ev.dt
  GROUP BY o.team, ev.dt
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE ops_reports.team_failures PARTITION (dt)
SELECT p.team,
       CASE p.team WHEN 'data' THEN 'Data platform' WHEN 'web' THEN 'Web' ELSE 'Other' END AS team_name,
       p.events,
       p.fails,
       p.dt
FROM per_team p;

-- The Hive of mart.team_failures, generated by export_hive() from the logic cell of
-- mart__team_failures.ipynb, beside this file. Edit the logic there, not this file: each
-- export_hive() writes it again, from the notebook as last saved. It holds 2 steps, in
-- the order they are sent: create() and write().

-- 1 of 2: create()
CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mart.team_failures (
  team STRING,
  team_name STRING,
  events BIGINT,
  fails BIGINT,
  PRIMARY KEY (team, dt) DISABLE NOVALIDATE
)
PARTITIONED BY (
  dt STRING
)
STORED AS ORC
LOCATION '/data/mart.db/team_failures';

-- 2 of 2: write()
-- The Session setting "dynamic_partitions": this write fills dt from its rows.
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition=true;
SET hive.exec.dynamic.partition.mode=nonstrict;
WITH ev AS (
  SELECT
    e.job_id,
    e.dt,
    e.event_type
  FROM ops.job_events AS e
  WHERE
    e.dt BETWEEN '2026-09-20' AND '2026-09-24'
    AND e.event_type IN ('start', 'retry', 'fail', 'finish')
), per_team AS (
  SELECT
    o.team,
    ev.dt,
    COUNT(*) AS events,
    SUM(CASE WHEN ev.event_type = 'fail' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS fails
  FROM ev
  JOIN ops.job_owners AS o
    ON o.job_id = ev.job_id AND o.dt = ev.dt
  GROUP BY
    o.team,
    ev.dt
)
INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE mart.team_failures PARTITION(dt)
SELECT
  p.team,
  CASE p.team WHEN 'data' THEN 'Data platform' WHEN 'web' THEN 'Web' ELSE 'Other' END AS team_name,
  p.events,
  p.fails,
  p.dt
FROM per_team AS p;

tables/mart__team_failures/mart__team_failures.ipynb

mart.team_failures

A Saved table made by the batch team_failures, with project.convert(...), from the statements in conversion/statement.hql, as its script wrote them. Its logic cell builds the same query from the Toolbox's own functions, with create(), preview() and write().

Terms

  • Saved table: a table in the warehouse that only your own code writes, its files in a folder of its own.
  • Logic cell: the code cell whose first line is # logic: the table's imports and functions alone.
  • Decision: one thing about this table that only you decide, such as its location or its Key; conversion/checklist.md lists each one still open.
  • Key: the columns whose values name exactly one row of the table.
  • Original: the table the converted script writes, in the warehouse already, which this table copies, or, for a table made from a query, that query. conversion/in_dev.hql holds the original's Hive, which the cells comparing it read.

Steps

1. Answer its Decisions. Goal: each Decision made. Action: answer each open Decision conversion/checklist.md lists in the decisions cell of admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb. Done when conversion/checklist.md says None under Open Decisions, once that Workflow's next step writes your answers in; then step 2. 2. Run the cells. Goal: its rows, its Hive and its lineage, tried. Action: run the code cells top to bottom. Done when each runs with no error.

Decision rules

  • If you still want to compare it with the original, keep the cells comparing them.
  • If you don't, delete those cells: they stay until you delete them.

Self-check

  • conversion/checklist.md says None under Open Decisions.
  • Each code cell runs top to bottom with no error.

Code cell 2

# logic
from sqlglot_composer import (
    AS,
    Clause,
    FROM,
    GROUP_BY,
    JOIN,
    SELECT,
    Statement,
    WHERE,
    all_of,
    between,
    count_rows,
    derived,
    equals,
    if_else,
    is_in,
    mapped,
    statement,
    sum_of,
)
from references import ops
from sqlglot_composer import create_table  # for create()
from sqlglot_composer import INSERT_OVERWRITE, Statement, statement  # for write()
from references.mart__team_failures import team_failures  # the Saved table's own Table reference

EVENT_TYPE_VALUES = ["start", "retry", "fail", "finish"]
TEAM_NAME_BY_TEAM = {
    "data": "Data platform",
    "web": "Web",
}

e = AS(ops.job_events, "e")
ev = derived("ev", statement(
    SELECT(
        e.job_id,
        e.dt,
        e.event_type,
    ),
    FROM(e),
    WHERE(all_of(
        between(e.dt, "2026-09-20", "2026-09-24"),
        is_in(e.event_type, EVENT_TYPE_VALUES),
    )),
))
o = AS(ops.job_owners, "o")
per_team = derived("per_team", statement(
    SELECT(
        o.team,
        ev.dt,
        AS(count_rows(), "events"),
        AS(sum_of(if_else(equals(ev.event_type, "fail"), 1, 0)), "fails"),
    ),
    FROM(ev),
    JOIN(o, ON=all_of(
        equals(o.job_id, ev.job_id),
        equals(o.dt, ev.dt),
    ), reads_all_partitions=True),
    GROUP_BY(o.team, ev.dt),
))
p = AS(per_team, "p")


def rows() -> list[Clause]:
    """The clauses of the query, which converted() and a write share."""
    return [
        SELECT(
            p.team,
            AS(mapped(p.team, TEAM_NAME_BY_TEAM, otherwise="Other"), "team_name"),
            p.events,
            p.fails,
            p.dt,
        ),
        FROM(p),
    ]


def converted() -> Statement:
    """The query, as the Hive this Conversion was made from writes it."""
    return statement(*rows())


def create() -> Statement:
    """Create mart.team_failures from its Table reference, if it isn't there yet."""
    return create_table(team_failures, may_exist=True)


def preview() -> Statement:
    """The rows write() would save, as a SELECT to run and check first."""
    return converted()


def write() -> Statement:
    """Save the rows into mart.team_failures, as the original's INSERT did."""
    return statement(INSERT_OVERWRITE(team_failures), *rows())

warehouse.py

Show its 86 lines
"""warehouse - connect, which takes your connection, and its two calls.

Why: every notebook in the Project reaches the warehouse through query and execute, so a change
to how you reach it is made here, once.

Each notebook runs on its own, so each gives its connection at its top, in its setup cell, with
connect(your_connection), before it calls query or execute; until it does, they refuse. Your
connection is an object whose query(sql) runs Hive that gives back rows and gives them back as
a pandas DataFrame, and whose execute(sql) runs Hive that changes something: whatever it gives
back, execute drops. You pick which of query and execute sends each Statement, by calling
run_query(s, query) for what gives back rows, as a SELECT does, or run_execute(s, execute)
for what changes something, as a write does; a Scheduled run's Run cell,
built.run(query, execute), picks for each of its Statements the same way. You edit nothing
here, unless your connection names its two calls another way: then change the calls query and
execute make below, and the names connect checks for, ("query", "execute"), to yours. Let your
connection's two calls share one session, so a Session setting sent through execute holds for
what is sent after it; and let each raise when the warehouse reports a failure, since the
Toolbox knows a step failed only when your connection raises. Keep it importing nothing from
references/, building_blocks/ or tables/, so any notebook or script of yours can import it.
"""

from typing import NoReturn

import pandas as pd

# The connection this notebook gave connect(...): None until it connects.
_connection = None


def connect(connection: object) -> None:
    """Set the connection query and execute pass each Hive string to, such as connect(conn),
    where conn is your team's connection: an object with a query(sql) and an execute(sql)."""
    global _connection
    missing = [call for call in ("query", "execute")
               if not callable(getattr(connection, call, None))]
    if missing:
        calls = " or ".join(f"{call}(sql)" for call in missing)
        _refuse(
            what=f"The connection given to connect(...), a {type(connection).__name__}, "
            f"has no {calls} to call.",
            why="query passes each Hive string that gives back rows to your connection's "
            "query(sql), and execute each one that changes something to its execute(sql), "
            "so your connection needs both.",
            fix="Pass connect(...) the connection itself, the object with both, such as "
            "connect(conn), where conn is your team's connection. If yours names them another "
            "way, change the functions in your Project's warehouse.py: the names connect "
            "checks for, (\"query\", \"execute\"), and the calls query and execute make.",
            error=TypeError,
        )
    _connection = connection


def query(hive: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Run a Hive string that gives back rows, and give them back as a pandas DataFrame."""
    _check_connected("query")
    return _connection.query(hive)


def execute(hive: str) -> None:
    """Run a Hive string that changes something, such as a write, and give back nothing."""
    _check_connected("execute")
    _connection.execute(hive)


def _check_connected(call: str) -> None:
    """Refuse a call to query or execute made before this notebook connected."""
    if _connection is None:
        _refuse(
            what=f"No connection: call connect(...) first. {call}(...) was called before this "
            "notebook gave warehouse.py its connection.",
            why="Each notebook runs on its own, in a Python of its own, so each connects at "
            "its top: a connection given in another notebook, or before this one's kernel "
            "restarted, isn't this one's.",
            fix="In this notebook's setup cell, at its top, fill in the line connect(...) "
            "under its from warehouse import line with your connection, such as connect(conn), "
            "where conn is your team's connection, then run that cell again.",
            error=RuntimeError,
        )


def _refuse(what: str, why: str, fix: str, error: type[Exception]) -> NoReturn:
    """Stop, with the four parts every refusal of the Toolbox has."""
    raise error(f"\n  What happened:  {what}"
                f"\n  Why it matters: {why}"
                f"\n  Usual fix:      {fix}"
                "\n  Opt-out:        none - this one can't be switched off.")
